# Credit terms: tester read-through (26 Oct 2026)

QA lead, 26 Oct 2026: *"The purpose of editing the test case is to make sure that the preconditions/steps/and expected behavior are related and the one who is running the test cases as a manual QA tester may understand the test scenario."* and *"Make sure that your work does not bite me"*. Brief: `build/tester-readthrough-2026-10-26/WORKER-BRIEF.md`. DRAFT only: nothing written to TestRail, Jira, Slack or Google; nothing committed.

Generated by `build/tester-readthrough-2026-10-26/credit-terms/verify_and_report.py` from the live read-backs (`build/credit-terms/applied/C<id>-after.json`, old) and `build/credit-terms/authoring/proposals.json` (new, after `authoring/build.py`). The fixes live in `build/credit-terms/authoring/readthrough.py` (applied by `ct_lib.render` after `pattern.py`).

## 1. Reading coverage
- Cases read in full as a tester (title, preconditions, steps, expected results, note): **334 of 334** (every remaining case; sections S1 34, S2 35, S3 26, S4 34, S5 24, S6 8, S7 12, S8 13, S9 10, S10 16, S11 20, S12 20, DATA 29, DESIGN 53).
- Read from `work/dump-before.txt` (3,684 lines, every line) and, after the fixes, every changed line in `work/review-after.txt`. Source sentences checked where a fix needed one: PRD v3 Story 8 (lines 739-790 of `sources/CONFLUENCE-963346440-v3-2026-10-23.md`).
- Keys removed from the library (deleted from TestRail on the QA lead's order today): **10**: S1-27 (C723891), S1-28 (C723892), S2-35 (C723935), S3-27 (C723963), S3-28 (C723964), S4-30 (C723994), S4-34 (C723998), S5-23 (C724023), S5-25 (C724025), S12-19 (C724126). Removed from the case modules, `scenarios.py`, `relevance.py`, `design_refs.py` and the fix-round list in `render_outputs.py`; `proposals.json`, the setup file and the outputs no longer contain them (checked by this script).
- Requirements those cases covered: each one covered only by them now reads "No manual case: deleted 26 Oct 2026 on the QA lead's order (a manual tester cannot set it up); left to the developers' own checks" in REPORT.md §2 (S1-E3 (S1-27), EH-S1 (S1-28), UFS-1 (S1-28), UFS-31 (S2-35), UFS-21 (S3-27), UFS-26 (S3-28), EH-S4c (S4-34), UFS-38 (S4-34), S5-E3 (S5-23), EH-S5b (S5-25), UFS-45 (S5-25), EH-S12 (S12-19)). Requirements those cases quoted that remaining cases still cover: EH-S2 (S2-30 to S2-34), KD-THREE (S1-15), EH-S3a (S3-25, S3-26), S3-R9 and S3-R9 LONG (S3-12 and others), S4-N1 (S4-26, S4-27, S4-28, D-48), UFS-51 (D-38). The coverage gate in `authoring/build.py` accepts the exclusion (`ct_lib.NO_MANUAL`) and fails if a deleted key comes back or an excluded item is quoted again.

## 2. Gates and identity
- `python3 build/credit-terms/authoring/build.py`: **PROBLEM lines: 0** (every earlier gate kept; new: no "mark this test Blocked" line or variant in any field or in the authoring data that feeds the setup file; rendered steps checked for two UI actions; a result never points at a step the case does not have; the identity baseline is now the live read-back, `applied/C<id>-after.json`).
- Quotes block onward (quotes, From lines, build line, References from Design, AUTOMATION line) byte-identical to `applied/C<id>-after.json`: **333 of 334**; the other 1 differ only by the added quote listed below (identical once it is taken out). Titles unchanged: 334 of 334.
  - Added quote, S8-08 (C724055): **S8-R1:** "When a record is sent to QuickBooks with a term QuickBooks does not have, ShopView first creates the term in QuickBooks under the same name, then sends the record. This is how the standard terms already work today." (under the existing From: line for PRD v3, so no new From line is needed).
- Identity problems: **0**.

## 3. What changed, per case
Check numbers are the brief's: 1 clear setup · 2 no vague words · 3 a negative check proves its trigger ran · 4 one action per step, no repeats · 5 related / visible · 6 understandable · 7 no Blocked line · 8 enrolment (A/R and A/P only).

Every one of the 334 cases failed check 7 (the line "If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done"." closed every case's preconditions) and has it removed. |---|---|
| D-01, D-11, D-12, D-13, D-16, D-17, D-19 | The bullet "…this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here": the drawn picture and the build picture disagree on heading style, Cancel style, × colour and ticked-box style, and the sources give no single look until Chris Ward answers question 12. |
| S5-16 | "The window looks as it did before the release." The sources say the window is as today and give no list of today's fields; the two concrete bullets (neither Credit Terms nor Credit Limit shows) carry the check. |
| S5-07, S5-08 | "Choose to add a new customer from the customer picker." The PRD names the New Customer window but not the picker's own label for adding a customer. |
| S8-04 | "Send vendor invoice A to QuickBooks the way the shop does today." The PRD says only "is sent to QuickBooks"; it does not name the screen or button. |
| S8-09 | "…whose invoice QuickBooks will refuse for a reason the sandbox gives today" and "Fix the cause in the sandbox." The PRD names no refusal reason; the setup file's example (a tax code the sandbox does not accept) is only an example. |
| S11-13 | Step 1 "Note the Net 30 row's Customers count." / "It includes customer A.": a count cannot show which customers it includes; customer A being counted under Net 30 is seen in Move Customers (another case's subject), and the sources give no other on-screen way. |
| S4-14, S9-07 | "the existing Credit Hold notice shows": the PRD keeps today's notice without quoting its words (the build fact "Cannot invoice this customer. Customer is on Credit Hold" is not a source). |

## 5. Negative checks without a control
Controls added (check 3): **S8-08** (term B, used on an invoice that has synced, must appear in QuickBooks' Terms list while term A must not; PRD S8-R1 quoted) and **S8-06** (an invoice of customer A syncs after the move before the QuickBooks customer is checked; backed by the already-quoted S8-R6, "new invoices still carry the right term", so no new quote).

| Cases | Why no control was added |
|---|---|
| S8-07, S8-07b | Deactivating, reactivating, editing or deleting a term "sends nothing to QuickBooks" (S8-R7): nothing is sent, so there is no sync to wait for and no sync log the sources name. The trigger is proven on screen (the toasts). |
| S8-10 | Without the integration nothing is sent (S8-N1); there is no sync to prove. The trigger (the invoice is created) is a step result. |
| S11-16 | AccountingHub's Payment Terms list is untouched (OOS): no source names a sync between ShopView terms and that list; the trigger (term created, toast) is a step result. |
| S11-18, S12-13, S12-13b, S12-13c, S12-17 | "No log row / no note": the log and notes are written at the moment of the action, the trigger is a step result, and the newest row is compared with one written down first. No source sentence gives a separate signal that the log is working (S12-14 has one: user B's row). |
| S11-14 | "Nothing is re-sent at release": compared with captures taken before the release on production; no source gives a sync log. |
| S2-27, S3-21, S4-18, S4-19 | Double-click cases: the single toast and the single log row/note are the control; no change needed. |

## 6. Enrolment (check 8)
Check 8 applies to A/R and A/P only. In Credit terms the lines about a second location are kept where a step switches location or a result depends on another location's calendar: S1-21, S3-06, S7-04 (user B now signed in at location B in a second browser), S12-10, DATA-13, DATA-18. Nothing else names enrolment.

## 7. Cases a manual tester still cannot set up (for the QA lead; not deleted, not changed in meaning)
- **S12-18** (C724125) "The log is refused by the server without Settings Finance": its only step is a request sent outside the page (marker HOLD), the same reason the ten cases above were deleted.
- **D-38** (C724185) "Design: Credit Terms Log load error looks as designed": needs the log to fail to load on demand ("Make sure the Credit Terms Log cannot be loaded on the test shop"), the same reason S12-19 was deleted.
- **S11-11** (C724096): an organization with no settings record cannot be made or recognised by a tester (marker HOLD, unchanged).

## 8. Old vs new text of every changed case
Old = the live read-back (`applied/C<id>-after.json`); new = `authoring/proposals.json`. Quotes, From lines, References from Design and AUTOMATION lines are not shown (identical, see §2).
### S1-02 (C723866) Both tabs show the same columns at the same widths
```
OLD
Title: Both tabs show the same columns at the same widths
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure at least one term is deactivated, term A.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Look at the column headings of the table on the Active tab.
  2. Click the "Inactive" tab.
Expected Result:

  Step 2: The Inactive tab shows term A in a table with the same four headings and the unlabeled "⋯" column, and every column is as wide as on the Active tab (the headings do not move when you switch tabs).

NEW
Title: Both tabs show the same columns at the same widths
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure at least one term is deactivated; call it term A.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Look at the column headings of the table on the Active tab.
  2. Click the "Inactive" tab.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The table has one heading row reading Name, Due, Customers and Vendors, followed by a column with no heading that holds a "⋯" button on every row. / There is one table only (no separate sections for standard terms and the shop's own terms).
  Step 2: The Inactive tab shows term A in a table with the same four headings and the unlabeled "⋯" column, and every column is as wide as on the Active tab (the headings do not move when you switch tabs).
```

### S1-06 (C723870) Clicking Customers sorts lowest count first, and again highest first
```
OLD
Title: Clicking Customers sorts lowest count first, and again highest first
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own terms A, B and C are held by exactly 1, 2 and 3 customers (new terms hold only this case's customers).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click the "Customers" column heading.
  2. Click the "Customers" column heading again.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The Customers column reads from top to bottom in rising order (every number is the same as or higher than the one above it), so term A (1) is above term B (2), which is above term C (3).
  Step 2: The order reverses: every number is the same as or lower than the one above it, and term C (3) is above term B (2), which is above term A (1).

NEW
Title: Clicking Customers sorts lowest count first, and again highest first
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Create three new terms of the shop's own for this test: term A held by exactly 1 customer, term B by 2 and term C by 3.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Click the "Customers" column heading.
  2. Click the "Customers" column heading again.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The Customers column reads from top to bottom in rising order (every number is the same as or higher than the one above it), so term A (1) is above term B (2), which is above term C (3).
  Step 2: The order reverses: every number is the same as or lower than the one above it, and term C (3) is above term B (2), which is above term A (1).
```

```
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
```

### S1-22 (C723886) Without Settings Finance a user cannot see or open Credit Terms
```
OLD
Title: Without Settings Finance a user cannot see or open Credit Terms
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as a user whose role has every Settings page except Finance.
  - Have the Credit Terms page's web address, copied earlier from an Admin's browser.
  - Start on the Settings page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Look at the Settings menu.
  2. Paste the Credit Terms page's web address into the browser's address bar.
  3. Press Enter.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: There is no Finance group (or it has no items), so neither "Payment Methods" nor "Credit Terms" shows.
  Step 2: The address is in the address bar.
  Step 3: The Credit Terms page does not show. / The app behaves as it does when this user opens the Payment Methods address (the same redirect or not-found page). / No "New Credit Term" button is reachable.

NEW
Title: Without Settings Finance a user cannot see or open Credit Terms
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as a user whose role has every Settings page except Finance.
  - Have the web addresses of the Credit Terms page and of the Payment Methods page, copied earlier from an Admin's browser.
  - Start on the Settings page.
Steps:
  1. Look at the Settings menu.
  2. Paste the Credit Terms page's web address into the browser's address bar.
  3. Press Enter.
  4. Paste the Payment Methods page's web address into the browser's address bar.
  5. Press Enter.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: There is no Finance group (or it has no items), so neither "Payment Methods" nor "Credit Terms" shows.
  Step 2: The address is in the address bar.
  Step 3: The Credit Terms page does not show. / No "New Credit Term" button is reachable.
  Step 4: The address is in the address bar.
  Step 5: The app behaves exactly as in step 3 (the same redirect or not-found page): this user is kept out of Payment Methods the same way as out of Credit Terms.
```

```
Title: A shop term held only by invoices says how many invoices

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer and no vendor today, but by five invoices created while their customer was on it (the customer was then moved to Net 30).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Hover "Edit".
  3. Hover "Move Customers".
  4. Hover "Delete Credit Term".
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu shows exactly these items, in this order: "Edit" (pencil, greyed out), "Make Default for New Customers" (circle check), "Move Customers" (person, greyed out), "Deactivate" (archive), "Delete Credit Term" (trash, in red, below a divider, greyed out). / Nothing else shows.
  Step 2: The hover reads "Used by 5 invoices" with "Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." below it.
  Step 3: The hover reads "No customers are on this term".
  Step 4: The hover reads "Used by 5 invoices" with "Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." below it.

NEW
Title: A shop term held only by invoices says how many invoices
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Create a new term of the shop's own for this test, term A, due 14 days after the invoice date.
  - Create a customer for this test on term A, and create five invoices for that customer.
  - Then change that customer's credit term to Net 30, so term A is held by no customer or vendor, only by the five invoices.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Hover "Edit".
  3. Hover "Move Customers".
  4. Hover "Delete Credit Term".
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu shows exactly these items, in this order: "Edit" (pencil, greyed out), "Make Default for New Customers" (circle check), "Move Customers" (person, greyed out), "Deactivate" (archive), "Delete Credit Term" (trash, in red, below a divider, greyed out). / Nothing else shows.
  Step 2: The hover reads "Used by 5 invoices" with "Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." below it.
  Step 3: The hover reads "No customers are on this term".
  Step 4: The hover reads "Used by 5 invoices" with "Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." below it.
```

### S2-31 (C723931) Deleting a term another user just put a customer on is refused
```
OLD
Title: Deleting a term another user just put a customer on is refused
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice.
  - Start with your browser showing the Credit Terms page and user B's browser showing Edit Customer for a customer who holds COD.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. In your browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  3. In user B's browser, set Credit Terms to term A.
  4. In user B's browser, click Save.
  5. In your browser, click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The delete confirmation opens.
  Step 3: Term A is selected in user B's Edit Customer.
  Step 4: The customer is saved on term A.
  Step 5: The app's error alert shows "Used by 1 customer. Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." The confirmation stays open and term A is not deleted.

NEW
Title: Deleting a term another user just put a customer on is refused
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice.
  - Create customer A for this test, on COD.
  - Start with your browser showing the Credit Terms page and user B's browser showing Edit Customer for customer A.
Steps:
  1. In your browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  3. In user B's browser, set Credit Terms to term A.
  4. In user B's browser, click Save.
  5. In your browser, click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The delete confirmation opens.
  Step 3: Term A is selected in user B's Edit Customer.
  Step 4: Customer A is saved on term A.
  Step 5: The app's error alert shows "Used by 1 customer. Deactivate it instead to take it out of the pickers." The confirmation stays open and term A is not deleted.
```
### S2-36 (C723936) Acting on a term another user renamed after the page loaded
```
OLD
Title: Acting on a term another user renamed after the page loaded
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice.
  - Choose a new name for term B (made for this test), that no term has.
  - Start with the Credit Terms page open on the Active tab in both browsers.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. In user B's browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. In user B's browser, click "Edit".
  3. In user B's browser, replace the Name with the name of term B.
  4. In user B's browser, click "Save Changes".
  5. In your browser, without reloading, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  6. Click "Make Default for New Customers".
  7. Click "Make Default".
Expected Result:
  Step 1: User B's menu opens.
  Step 2: User B's "Edit Credit Term" window opens.
  Step 3: The Name reads term B.
  Step 4: User B sees the toast "term B saved".
  Step 5: Your menu opens.
  Step 6: The confirmation opens.
  Step 7: The save is refused with the reason the PO gives (see the note). / The page keeps its state.
Note: The PRD says a change by another user is refused "with the reason in S2 Error Handling", but none of those texts covers a rename. Question 3 on Chris Ward's sheet (shared with him 23 Oct 2026) asks; this case is held until he answers.

NEW

Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.

  - Choose a new name for term A that no term has; call the new name term B.

Steps:
  1. In user B's browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. In user B's browser, click "Edit".
  3. In user B's browser, replace the Name with the name of term B.
  4. In user B's browser, click "Save Changes".
  5. In your browser, without reloading, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  6. Click "Make Default for New Customers".
  7. Click "Make Default".
Expected Result:
  Step 1: User B's menu opens.
  Step 2: User B's "Edit Credit Term" window opens.
  Step 3: The Name reads term B.
  Step 4: User B sees the toast "term B saved".
  Step 5: Your menu opens.
  Step 6: The confirmation opens.
  Step 7: The save is refused with the reason the PO gives (see the note). / The page keeps its state.
Note: The PRD says a change by another user is refused "with the reason in S2 Error Handling", but none of those texts covers a rename. Question 3 on Chris Ward's sheet (shared with him 23 Oct 2026) asks; this case is held until he answers.
```

### S3-03 (C723939) The blue line explains a Net term with today's date as the example
```
OLD
Title: The blue line explains a Net term with today's date as the example

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note today's date on the calendar of the test location (date A).

  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type "Net 14 Days" in Name.
  3. Type 14 in the Days box.
  4. Replace 14 with 0.
  5. Replace 0 with 15.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The "New Credit Term" window opens.
  Step 2: The Name reads Net 14 Days.
  Step 3: A blue information line shows below the options: "Net 14 Days means payment is due 14 days after the invoice date. An invoice dated date A is due date B." The dates read as a short month and day (for example "Oct 21" and "Nov 4").
  Step 4: The blue line disappears while the number is invalid.
  Step 5: The blue line comes back, now ending "is due" date A plus 15 days (for example Nov 5).

NEW

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note today's date on the calendar of the test location (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 14 days.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.

  Step 3: A blue information line shows below the options: "Net 14 Days means payment is due 14 days after the invoice date. An invoice dated date A is due date B." The dates read as a short month and day (for example "Oct 21" and "Nov 4").
  Step 4: The blue line disappears while the number is invalid.
  Step 5: The blue line comes back and now reads "… An invoice dated date A is due" followed by the date 15 days after date A (for example Nov 5).
```

```
  Step 5: Edit Customer opens.

  Step 7: Edit Vendor opens.
```

### S4-31 (C723995) A ticked customer another user moved meanwhile is left alone
```
OLD
Title: A ticked customer another user moved meanwhile is left alone
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 20 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly two customers, customer A and customer B.
  - Make sure Net 45 and Net 60 are active.
  - Create customer A and customer B just for this test, so no earlier test's changes affect them.
  - Start with your browser showing the Credit Terms page and user B's browser showing Edit Customer for customer B.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Choose "Net 45" in "Move To".
  4. In user B's browser, set Credit Terms to Net 60.
  5. In user B's browser, click Save.
  6. In your browser, click "Move 2 Customers".
  7. Open customer B's customer page.
  8. On the Credit Terms page, open the "⋯" page menu.
  9. Click "Audit Log".
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: A window titled "Move Customers from term A" opens.
  Step 3: The button reads "Move 2 Customers".
  Step 4: The dropdown shows Net 60.
  Step 5: Customer B is saved on Net 60.
  Step 6: The toast reads "1 customer moved to Net 45." (only customer A is counted).
  Step 7: Customer B is on Net 60 (left alone), with no move note.
  Step 8: The menu opens.
  Step 9: The new "Customers moved" row reads "1 customer from term A to Net 45".

NEW
Title: A ticked customer another user moved meanwhile is left alone

```

```
OLD
Title: Once another term is saved, the inactive term is no longer offered
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.

  - Make sure Net 30 is active.

  4. Open Edit Customer again.
  5. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
Expected Result:

  Step 2: The dropdown shows Net 30.
  Step 3: The customer saves on Net 30.
  Step 4: Credit Terms reads Net 30.
  Step 5: "Net 60" and "Net 60 (Inactive)" are not offered any more.

NEW

  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.

  - Then make sure Net 60 is deactivated.
  - Make sure Net 30 is active.

  2. Choose "Net 30" in Credit Terms.
  3. Click Save.
  4. Open Edit Customer again.
  5. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
Expected Result:

  Step 2: The dropdown shows Net 30.
  Step 3: The customer saves on Net 30.
  Step 4: Credit Terms reads Net 30.
  Step 5: "Net 60" and "Net 60 (Inactive)" are not offered any more.
```

```
  1. Set the invoice date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Save the vendor invoice.
  3. Change the invoice date to Oct 20, 2026.
  4. Save the vendor invoice.
  5. Change the vendor to vendor B.
  6. Save the vendor invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The due date shows Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  Step 2: It is saved with due date Oct 19, 2026.
  Step 3: The due date is recalculated to Nov 3, 2026 (Oct 20 + 14 days).
  Step 4: It is saved with due date Nov 3, 2026.
  Step 5: The due date is recalculated to Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days, Net 30).
  Step 6: The stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.

NEW
Title: A vendor invoice is due by the vendor's term and recalculated on change
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Create a new term of the shop's own for this test, term A, due 14 days after the invoice date, so a vendor on it has vendor invoices due 14 days after their date.
  - Create vendor A for this test, on term A, so the new vendor invoice starts on term A.
  - Create vendor B for this test, on Net 30.
  - Start with a new vendor invoice for vendor A open in Parts, Vendors, Vendor Invoices, not yet saved, so its due date follows vendor A's term.
Steps:
  1. Set the invoice date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Save the vendor invoice.
  3. Change the invoice date to Oct 20, 2026.
  4. Save the vendor invoice.
  5. Change the vendor to vendor B.
  6. Save the vendor invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The due date shows Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  Step 2: The vendor invoice is saved with due date Oct 19, 2026.
  Step 3: The due date is recalculated to Nov 3, 2026 (Oct 20 + 14 days).
  Step 4: The vendor invoice is saved with due date Nov 3, 2026.
  Step 5: The due date is recalculated to Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days, Net 30).
  Step 6: The stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.
```

### S8-01 (C724047) A shop's Net term reaches QuickBooks as a standard term on first use
```
OLD
Title: A shop's Net term reaches QuickBooks as a standard term on first use
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, is not in QuickBooks' Terms list.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete, for a customer who holds term A.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. On work order 1's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Wait until the invoice has synced to QuickBooks (it is no longer on the QB Unexported report).
  4. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  5. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The Due date reads Oct 19, 2026.
  Step 2: The invoice is created.
  Step 3: The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  Step 4: A term named exactly term A now exists, set as a standard term due in 14 days.
  Step 5: Its terms read term A and its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the same as in ShopView.

NEW

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Create a new term of the shop's own for this test, term A, due 14 days after the invoice date (it is not yet in QuickBooks' Terms list).
  - Create work order 1 for this test, complete, for a customer who holds term A.
Steps:
  1. On work order 1's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Wait until the invoice has synced to QuickBooks (it is no longer on the QB Unexported report).
  4. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  5. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The Due date reads Oct 19, 2026.
  Step 2: The invoice is created.
  Step 3: The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  Step 4: A term named exactly term A now exists, set as a standard term due in 14 days.
  Step 5: Its terms read term A and its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the same as in ShopView.
```

```
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
```

```
  - Create a new term of the shop's own for this test, term A, due 20 days after the invoice date.
  - Create customer A for this test on term A, and make sure it is in QuickBooks with terms term A.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - Create work order 1 for customer A, complete and ready to invoice, so an invoice for customer A can sync after the move.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Choose "Net 45" in "Move To".
  4. Click "Move 1 Customer".
  5. On work order 1's Finance tab, click Create Invoice.
  6. Wait until the invoice has synced to QuickBooks (it is no longer on the QB Unexported report).
  7. In QuickBooks, open customer A.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The "Move Customers from term A" window opens.
  Step 3: The dropdown shows Net 45.
  Step 4: The toast "1 customer moved to Net 45." shows.
  Step 5: The invoice is created on Net 45.
  Step 6: The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  Step 7: Customer A's terms still read term A: no customer update was sent, although a newer invoice of customer A has synced.
```

```
OLD
Title: Deactivating or reactivating a term sends nothing to QuickBooks
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.

Steps:

  2. Click "Deactivate".
  3. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  4. On the Inactive tab, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  5. Click "Reactivate".
  6. In QuickBooks, refresh the Terms list.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The toast "term A deactivated" shows.
  Step 3: Term A is still there, unchanged (active in QuickBooks).
  Step 4: The menu opens.
  Step 5: The term is active again.

NEW
```

### S8-09 (C724056) A QuickBooks refusal lands on the QB Unexported report with the reason
```
OLD
Title: A QuickBooks refusal lands on the QB Unexported report with the reason
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
```

### S11-09 (C724094) An old COD customer moved to Credit Hold gets $0, its limit kept in the note
```
OLD
Title: An old COD customer moved to Credit Hold gets $0, its limit kept in the note

Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "COD" row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Click "Select None".
  4. Type customer A's name in "Search customers".
  5. Tick customer A.
  6. Choose "Credit Hold" in "Move To".
  7. Click "Move 1 Customer".
  8. Click "Set Limits To $0 And Move".
  9. Open customer A's customer page.
  10. Click the "Notes" tab.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The window opens with every COD customer ticked.
  Step 3: The count reads "0 selected".
  Step 4: Only customer A is listed.
  Step 5: The count reads "1 selected".
  Step 6: The line "Their credit limit will be set to $0." shows.
  Step 7: The confirmation "Set 1 customer's credit limit to $0?" opens.
  Step 8: The toast "1 customer moved to Credit Hold." shows.
  Step 9: The "Credit term" reads Credit Hold and the credit limit reads $0.00.
  Step 10: The note reads "Credit term changed from COD to Credit Hold by your user name (Move Customers). Credit limit $1,000.00 → $0."

NEW
Title: An old COD customer moved to Credit Hold gets $0, its limit kept in the note
Preconditions:

  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:

  2. Click "Move Customers".

  4. Type customer A's name in "Search customers".
  5. Tick customer A.

  10. Click the "Notes" tab.

  Step 2: The "Move Customers from COD" window opens with every COD customer ticked.
  Step 3: The count reads "0 selected".
  Step 4: Only customer A is listed.
  Step 5: The count reads "1 selected".
  Step 6: The line "Their credit limit will be set to $0." shows.
  Step 7: The confirmation "Set 1 customer's credit limit to $0?" opens.
  Step 8: The toast "1 customer moved to Credit Hold." shows.
```

### S11-12 (C724097) A credit memo's due date on a statement counts on the location's calendar
```
OLD
Title: A credit memo's due date on a statement counts on the location's calendar
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer A holds Net 30 at the test location, whose time zone has daylight saving time, and has a credit memo dated Oct 20, 2026.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Open customer A's statement.
  2. Open the customer's unpaid transactions list.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days; the Nov 1 clock change does not move it). / Before the release this screen could show one day less. / The later date is the correct one.
  Step 2: The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026, the same.

NEW
Title: A credit memo's due date on a statement counts on the location's calendar
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test location's time zone has daylight saving time.
  - Have customer A on Net 30 at the test location, with a credit memo dated Oct 20, 2026.
Steps:
  1. Open customer A's statement.
  2. Open the customer's unpaid transactions list.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days; the Nov 1 clock change does not move it).
  Step 2: The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026, the same.
Note: Before the release this screen could show one day less; the later date is the correct one.
```

```
  Step 3: The window closes and the customer is saved on Net 45.

NEW

  - Create customer A for this test, on Net 30.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - Write down the newest row of the Credit Terms Log before you start.
Steps:
  1. Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  2. Choose "Net 45" in Credit Terms.
  3. Click Save.
  4. Click the "Notes" tab.
  5. Open the Credit Terms page.

  Step 2: The dropdown shows Net 45.
  Step 3: The window closes and the customer is saved on Net 45.
  Step 4: No new note about the credit term was written (only Move Customers writes one).
```

### S12-11 (C724116) Searching the log matches Event, User or Details, ignoring capitals
```
OLD
Title: Searching the log matches Event, User or Details, ignoring capitals
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note your user name.
  - Make sure the log holds rows for several events, including at least one "Default changed" row and one row whose Details mention Net 45.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  2. Click "Audit Log".
  3. Type "default changed" in the search box.
  4. Replace the text with "NET 45".
  5. Replace the text with your user name in lower-case letters.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The "Credit Terms Log" window opens.
  Step 3: Only "Default changed" rows remain.
  Step 4: Only rows whose Event, User or Details contain Net 45 remain.
  Step 5: Only rows whose User is your user name remain.

NEW
Title: Searching the log matches Event, User or Details, ignoring capitals
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note your user name.
  - Make sure the log holds at least one "Default changed" row (if it does not, make another term the default and then make the earlier default the default again).
  - Make sure the log holds at least one row you made whose Details mention Net 45 (if it does not, deactivate Net 45 and then reactivate it).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  2. Click "Audit Log".
  3. Type "default changed" in the search box.
  4. Replace the text with "NET 45".
  5. Replace the text with your user name in lower-case letters.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The "Credit Terms Log" window opens.
  Step 3: Only "Default changed" rows remain.
  Step 4: Only rows whose Event, User or Details contain Net 45 remain.
  Step 5: Only rows whose User is your user name remain.
```

```
  Step 1: The invoice preview shows Invoice Date and Due date.
```
```
  Step 2: The Due date reads Nov 10, 2026 (day 10 of November).
```

### DATA-18 (C724145) A vendor invoice from a delivery uses the delivery's location calendar
```
OLD
Title: A vendor invoice from a delivery uses the delivery's location calendar
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) holds Net 30.
  - Make sure a delivery for vendor A belongs to location B, in a time zone where the date is already one day later than where you work. Run the case at a time of day when the two dates differ.
  - Note today's date at location B (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 30 days.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Receive the delivery for vendor A.
  2. Open the new vendor invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The delivery is received and its vendor invoice is created.
  Step 2: Its due date reads date B, counted from location B's date A (not from your location's date).

NEW
Title: A vendor invoice from a delivery uses the delivery's location calendar
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Create vendor A for this test, on Net 30.
  - Have a delivery for vendor A, not yet received, at location B, a location whose time zone is already one day later than where you work.
  - Run the case at a time of day when the two dates differ.
  - Note today's date at location B (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 30 days.
Steps:
  1. Receive the delivery for vendor A.
  2. Open the new vendor invoice.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The delivery is received and its vendor invoice is created.
  Step 2: Its due date reads date B, counted from location B's date A (not from your location's date).
```
### DATA-19 (C724146) The blue line's due date is exact for Net 365 and day 31
```
OLD
Title: The blue line's due date is exact for Net 365 and day 31
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note today's date at the test location (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 365 days.
  - Work out date C: day 31 of the month after date A, or that month's last day if it is shorter.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type 365 in the Days box.
  3. Click the Day box.
  4. Type 31 in the Day box.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The window opens.
  Step 2: The blue line ends "An invoice dated date A is due date B."
  Step 3: The "Due day of the following month" option is selected.
  Step 4: The blue line ends "An invoice dated date A is due date C." (for example Oct 23 → Nov 30, because November has 30 days).

NEW
```

### DATA-20 (C724147) Customers counts include odd spellings and every status, never Counter Sale
```
OLD
Title: Customers counts include odd spellings and every status, never Counter Sale
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test) is held by no customer.
  - Before you start, write down how many customers hold Net 30 (number B; the Counter Sale customer is never counted).
  - Have a contacts import file with three new customers: customer B with the credit term text "NET 30", one with "net30" and one with term A written in capital letters.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Import the file through Settings, Data Import, Contacts.
  2. Reload the Credit Terms page.
  3. Set customer B's status to inactive on its customer page.
  4. Reload the Credit Terms page.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The three customers are created (none is refused).
  Step 2: Net 30 reads number B + 2 (for example 40 + 2 = 42): "NET 30" and "net30" both count as Net 30. / Term A reads 1.
  Step 3: The customer is inactive.
  Step 4: Net 30 still reads number B + 2: customers count whatever their status.

NEW
```

```
Title: Vendors counts follow the same rules as customers
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) is stored with "NET 45" and another vendor holds Net 45 and is inactive.
  - Before you start, write down how many vendors hold Net 45 (number A, which includes vendor A and the inactive vendor).
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Look at the Net 45 row's Vendors cell.
  2. Open Edit Vendor for vendor A.
  3. Choose "Net 60".
  4. Click Save & Close.
  5. Reload the Credit Terms page.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: It reads number A (for example 5): "NET 45" counts as Net 45 and the inactive vendor counts.
  Step 2: The window opens.
  Step 3: The dropdown shows Net 60.
  Step 4: The window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  Step 5: Net 45 reads number A − 1 (for example 4) and Net 60's Vendors count went up by 1.

NEW
Title: Vendors counts follow the same rules as customers
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have vendor A, from before the release, stored with the credit term text "NET 45".
  - Make sure another vendor holds Net 45 and is inactive.
  - Before you start, write down how many vendors hold Net 45 (number A, which includes vendor A and the inactive vendor).
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
Steps:
  1. Look at the Net 45 row's Vendors cell.
  2. Open Edit Vendor for vendor A.
  3. Choose "Net 60".
  4. Click Save & Close.
  5. Reload the Credit Terms page.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The Net 45 row's Vendors cell reads number A (for example 5): "NET 45" counts as Net 45 and the inactive vendor counts.
  Step 2: The Edit Vendor window for vendor A opens.
  Step 3: The dropdown shows Net 60.
  Step 4: The window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  Step 5: Net 45 reads number A − 1 (for example 4) and Net 60's Vendors count went up by 1.
```
### DATA-23 (C724151) Customers counts update straight after a move
```
OLD
Title: Customers counts update straight after a move
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.

  - Before you start, write down how many customers hold Net 15 (number A) and how many hold Net 20 (number B).
  - Create customer A, customer B and customer C just for this test, so no earlier test's changes affect them.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 15" row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Click "Select None".
  4. Tick customer A.
  5. Tick customer B.
  6. Tick customer C.
  7. Choose "Net 20" in "Move To".
  8. Click "Move 3 Customers".
  9. Reload the page.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The menu opens.
  Step 2: The window shows "number A selected" (for example "7 selected").
  Step 3: The count reads "0 selected".
  Step 4: The count reads "1 selected".
  Step 5: The count reads "2 selected".
  Step 6: The count reads "3 selected". / The button reads "Move 3 Customers".
  Step 7: The dropdown shows Net 20.
  Step 8: The toast reads "3 customers moved to Net 20." and, without reloading, Net 15 reads number A − 3 (for example 4) and Net 20 reads number B + 3 (for example 5).
  Step 9: The counts are the same after the reload.

NEW
```

### DATA-24 (C724152) Tab counts change by one on each deactivate, reactivate, create and delete
```
OLD
Title: Tab counts change by one on each deactivate, reactivate, create and delete
Preconditions:
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - Before you start, write down the Active tab's count (number A) and the Inactive tab's count (number B).
  - Choose a name for term A that no term has.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
Steps:
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type the name of term A in Name.
  3. Type 14 in the Days box.
  4. Click "Create Credit Term".
  5. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 45" row.
  6. Click "Deactivate".
  7. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  8. Click "Deactivate".
  9. Click the "Inactive" tab.
  10. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 45" row.
  11. Click "Reactivate".
  12. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  13. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  14. Click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.
Expected Result:
  Step 1: The window opens.
  Step 2: The field shows term A.
  Step 3: The blue information line shows.
  Step 4: Active reads number A + 1 (for example 13) and Inactive number B.
  Step 5: The menu opens.
  Step 6: Active reads number A (back to the starting number) and Inactive number B + 1.
  Step 7: The menu opens.
  Step 8: Active number A − 1, Inactive number B + 2.
  Step 9: Net 45 and term A are among its rows.
  Step 10: The menu opens.
  Step 11: Active number A, Inactive number B + 1.
  Step 12: The menu opens.
  Step 13: The confirmation opens.
  Step 14: Active number A and Inactive number B: both counts are back where they started (for example 12 and 0).

NEW

  9. Click the "Inactive" tab.
  10. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 45" row.
  11. Click "Reactivate".
  12. Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
```

```
  Step 3: A dark grey box opens under the field reading "COD and Credit Hold customers can't charge to account, so they have no credit limit." (as in ct-p6-06-edit-customer-cod-or-credit-hold-locks-the-limit.png).
  Step 4: At 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll. / The layout described above is unchanged, only wider.

NEW
```

```
  Step 2: At 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll. / The layout described above is unchanged, only wider.
```
```
Note: The PRD writes "Over limit"; today's badge reads "Over Limit" and the PRD keeps the existing badge (register CT-D07, settled).
```
